It's just that I get stuck and can't get my body to move the way it's supposed to in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen after a stressful event or when you feel very anxious, and do you ever lose time or have gaps in your memory for what happened during them?\n\nPatient: This all really started after I responded to a chaotic multi-car crash on I-95 about ten months ago. I don't lose time or have gaps in my memory during these moments; I'm actually fully aware of what's happening. I just feel completely frozen and can't get my body to snap into action.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you have nightmares, flashbacks, or get very upset or on edge when something reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden panic when I'm reminded of the crash. It's more that ever since that day, I've just felt generally off, disorganized, and disconnected from everything.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you ever suddenly feel like you are back at the scene, or have episodes where you lose awareness of what is happening or cannot remember parts of the event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm suddenly back at the crash scene, and I don't experience flashbacks like that. I also don't have any gaps in my memory; I can remember the entire event clearly.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you get intrusive memories, nightmares, or sudden physical distress when something reminds you of it, even if you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get intrusive memories, nightmares, or physical panic when I think about the crash. For me, that accident just seemed to be the point where I started feeling \"off,\" disorganized, and increasingly disconnected. I don't feel distressed by the memory of the event itself.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you had any episodes where you suddenly lose awareness of what is happening around you, feel like you are in a dream, or have gaps in your memory for parts of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm in a dream and I don't have any gaps in my memory. I'm completely aware of everything going on around me, but I just feel frozen, slow, and like my body is stuck on a different channel.\n\nDoctor: When you feel \"stuck\" or out of sync, do you ever completely lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't lose awareness, have blank spells, or forget anything at all. I'm actually completely aware of what's happening around me, and I can see my crew reacting to how off I'm acting. I just feel frozen, like my body is on a different channel and I can't snap myself into the right gear to respond."

It's just that I get stuck and can't get my body to move the way it's supposed to in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen after a stressful event or when you feel very anxious, and do you ever lose time or have gaps in your memory for what happened during them?\n\nPatient: This all really started after I responded to a chaotic multi-car crash on I-95 about ten months ago. I don't lose time or have gaps in my memory during these moments; I'm actually fully aware of what's happening. I just feel completely frozen and can't get my body to snap into action.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you have nightmares, flashbacks, or get very upset or on edge when something reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden panic when I'm reminded of the crash. It's more that ever since that day, I've just felt generally off, disorganized, and disconnected from everything.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you ever suddenly feel like you are back at the scene, or have episodes where you lose awareness of what is happening or cannot remember parts of the event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm suddenly back at the crash scene, and I don't experience flashbacks like that. I also don't have any gaps in my memory; I can remember the entire event clearly.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you get intrusive memories, nightmares, or sudden physical distress when something reminds you of it, even if you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get intrusive memories, nightmares, or physical panic when I think about the crash. For me, that accident just seemed to be the point where I started feeling \"off,\" disorganized, and increasingly disconnected. I don't feel distressed by the memory of the event itself.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you had any episodes where you suddenly lose awareness of what is happening around you, feel like you are in a dream, or have gaps in your memory for parts of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm in a dream and I don't have any gaps in my memory. I'm completely aware of everything going on around me, but I just feel frozen, slow, and like my body is stuck on a different channel.\n\nDoctor: When you feel \"stuck\" or out of sync, do you ever completely lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't lose awareness, have blank spells, or forget anything at all. I'm actually completely aware of what's happening around me, and I can see my crew reacting to how off I'm acting. I just feel frozen, like my body is on a different channel and I can't snap myself into the right gear to respond.\n\nDoctor: When you feel frozen or out of sync, do you ever lose track of time, have blank spells, or later not remember what happened during the episode?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience any blank spells or memory loss when this happens. I'm completely aware of everything around me, I just feel totally unable to get my body to move or react in sync with the situation."

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: On calls I feel like I’m moving wrong and doing the wrong things, like my actions don’t match what’s happening around me. My crew says I’m acting really off, and I just don't know what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been hearing or seeing things that other people do not, or feeling like others can read, control, or put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been hearing or seeing anything unusual like that. And I don't feel like anyone is reading my mind or putting thoughts into my head either.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you ever feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, like you are watching yourself from outside your body or the world feels unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or that the world is unreal. It's more like my body is just on a different channel and completely out of sync, so I can't get my actions to match what's happening around me. I just get stuck and can't seem to snap into the right gear.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you also have any episodes of weakness, paralysis, numbness, trouble speaking, shaking, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any weakness, numbness, shaking, or seizures. Physically, everything seems to work fine. It's just that I get stuck and can't get my body to move the way it's supposed to in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen after a stressful event or when you feel very anxious, and do you ever lose time or have gaps in your memory for what happened during them?\n\nPatient: This all really started after I responded to a chaotic multi-car crash on I-95 about ten months ago. I don't lose time or have gaps in my memory during these moments; I'm actually fully aware of what's happening. I just feel completely frozen and can't get my body to snap into action.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you have nightmares, flashbacks, or get very upset or on edge when something reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden panic when I'm reminded of the crash. It's more that ever since that day, I've just felt generally off, disorganized, and disconnected from everything.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you ever suddenly feel like you are back at the scene, or have episodes where you lose awareness of what is happening or cannot remember parts of the event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm suddenly back at the crash scene, and I don't experience flashbacks like that. I also don't have any gaps in my memory; I can remember the entire event clearly.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you get intrusive memories, nightmares, or sudden physical distress when something reminds you of it, even if you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get intrusive memories, nightmares, or physical panic when I think about the crash. For me, that accident just seemed to be the point where I started feeling \"off,\" disorganized, and increasingly disconnected. I don't feel distressed by the memory of the event itself.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you had any episodes where you suddenly lose awareness of what is happening around you, feel like you are in a dream, or have gaps in your memory for parts of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm in a dream and I don't have any gaps in my memory. I'm completely aware of everything going on around me, but I just feel frozen, slow, and like my body is stuck on a different channel.\n\nDoctor: When you feel \"stuck\" or out of sync, do you ever completely lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't lose awareness, have blank spells, or forget anything at all. I'm actually completely aware of what's happening around me, and I can see my crew reacting to how off I'm acting. I just feel frozen, like my body is on a different channel and I can't snap myself into the right gear to respond.\n\nDoctor: When you feel frozen or out of sync, do you ever lose track of time, have blank spells, or later not remember what happened during the episode?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience any blank spells or memory loss when this happens. I'm completely aware of everything around me, I just feel totally unable to get my body to move or react in sync with the situation.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel like your mind is separate from your body or that your body is acting on its own, even though you are fully aware of what is happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really feels like my body is on a completely different channel. I'll just stand there staring at my hands or doing something weirdly slow like re-rolling bandages while everyone else is rushing around me. I know exactly what's happening, but I just can't get my body to snap into the right gear.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: On calls I feel like I’m moving wrong and doing the wrong things, like my actions don’t match what’s happening around me. My crew says I’m acting really off, and I just don't know what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been hearing or seeing things that other people do not, or feeling like others can read, control, or put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been hearing or seeing anything unusual like that. And I don't feel like anyone is reading my mind or putting thoughts into my head either.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you ever feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, like you are watching yourself from outside your body or the world feels unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or that the world is unreal. I don't lose time or have gaps in my memory during these moments; I'm actually fully aware of what's happening. I just feel completely frozen and can't get my body to snap into action.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you have nightmares, flashbacks, or get very upset or on edge when something reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden panic when I'm reminded of the crash. It's more that ever since that day, I've just felt generally off, disorganized, and disconnected from everything.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you ever suddenly feel like you are back at the scene, or have episodes where you lose awareness of what is happening or cannot remember parts of the event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm suddenly back at the crash scene, and I don't experience flashbacks like that. I also don't have any gaps in my memory; I can remember the entire event clearly.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you get intrusive memories, nightmares, or sudden physical distress when something reminds you of it, even if you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get intrusive memories, nightmares, or physical panic when I think about the crash. For me, that accident just seemed to be the point where I started feeling \"off,\" disorganized, and increasingly disconnected. I don't feel distressed by the memory of the event itself.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you had any episodes where you suddenly lose awareness of what is happening around you, feel like you are in a dream, or have gaps in your memory for parts of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm in a dream and I don't have any gaps in my memory.     "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: On calls I feel like I’m moving wrong and doing the wrong things, like my actions don’t match what’s happening around me. My crew says I’m acting really off, and I just don't know what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been hearing or seeing things that other people do not, or feeling like others can read, control, or put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been hearing or seeing anything unusual like that. And I don't feel like anyone is reading my mind or putting thoughts into my head either.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you ever feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, like you are watching yourself from outside your body or the world feels unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or that the world is unreal. It's more like my body is just on a different channel and completely out of sync, so I can't get my actions to match what's happening around me. I just get stuck and can't seem to snap into the right gear.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you also have any episodes of weakness, paralysis, numbness, trouble speaking, shaking, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any weakness, numbness, shaking, or seizures. Physically, everything seems to work fine. It's just that I get stuck and can't get my body to move the way it's supposed to in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen after a stressful event or when you feel very anxious, and do you ever lose time or have gaps in your memory for what happened during them?\n\nPatient: This all really started after I responded to a chaotic multi-car crash on I-95 about ten months ago. I don't lose time or have gaps in my memory during these moments; I'm actually fully aware of what's happening. I just feel completely frozen and can't get my body to snap into action.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you have nightmares, flashbacks, or get very upset or on edge when something reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden panic when I'm reminded of the crash. It's more that ever since that day, I've just felt generally off, disorganized, and disconnected from everything.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you ever suddenly feel like you are back at the scene, or have episodes where you lose awareness of what is happening or cannot remember parts of the event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm suddenly back at the crash scene, and I don't experience flashbacks like that. I also don't have any gaps in my memory; I can remember the entire event clearly.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, do you get intrusive memories, nightmares, or sudden physical distress when something reminds you of it, even if you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get intrusive memories, nightmares, or physical panic when I think about the crash. For me, that accident just seemed to be the point where I started feeling \"off,\" disorganized, and increasingly disconnected. I don't feel distressed by the memory of the event itself.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you had any episodes where you suddenly lose awareness of what is happening around you, feel like you are in a dream, or have gaps in your memory for parts of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm in a dream and I don't have any gaps in my memory. I'm completely aware of everything going on around me, but I just feel frozen, slow, and like my body is stuck on a different channel.\n\nDoctor: When you feel \"stuck\" or out of sync, do you ever completely lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't lose awareness, have blank spells, or forget anything at all. I'm actually completely aware of what's happening around me, and I can see my crew reacting to how off I'm acting. I just feel frozen, like my body is on a different channel and I can't snap myself into the right gear to respond.\n\nDoctor: When you feel frozen or out of sync, do you ever lose track of time, have blank spells, or later not remember what happened during the episode?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience any blank spells or memory loss when this happens. I'm completely aware of everything around me, I just feel totally unable to get my body to move or react in sync with the situation.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel like your mind is separate from your body or that your body is acting on its own, even though you are fully aware of what is happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really feels like my body is on a completely different channel. I'll just stand there staring at my hands or doing something weirdly slow like re-rolling bandages while everyone else is rushing around me. I know exactly what's happening, but I just can't get my body to snap into the right gear.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose awareness, have blank spells, or later not remember parts of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't lose awareness, have blank spells, or forget anything that happened. I am actually very aware of my surroundings during these times, but my body just feels frozen or like it's moving in slow motion. It's like my body is on a completely different channel and I can't snap myself into the right gear, even though I know exactly what is going on around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any true episodes of weakness, paralysis, numbness, shaking, or seizures, or is it only this feeling that your body is frozen or moving out of sync?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any actual seizures, numbness, or physical weakness. It really is just that feeling of my body being on a different channel, where I freeze up or move in slow motion and out of sync while everyone else is rushing around me.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen mainly when you are under stress or in emotionally charged situations, and do they improve when you are calm or away from the situation?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, because while the freezing up definitely happens during those chaotic calls, it doesn't really go away when things are calm. Even at home away from the stress of work, I still feel really flat, empty, and suspicious of what people are doing behind my back.\n\nDoctor: When you feel suspicious that people are doing things behind your back, do you think they are actually trying to harm, watch, or interfere with you, or does it feel more like a vague uneasiness without a clear belief?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely a specific belief rather than just a vague uneasiness. I am convinced that dispatch and some of the hospital staff are slipping hidden codes into the radio traffic and chart notes to track my mistakes. I believe they are actively building a case to get me fired.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are deliberately trying to harm, monitor, or sabotage you, and how certain are you that this is really happening?"

For the last couple of months, I've known that dispatch and hospital staff are slipping hidden codes into radio traffic and chart notes to track my mistakes and get me fired. Even though my supervisor swears it's just routine paperwork, I know what they are doing.

Even at home away from the stress of work, I still feel really flat, empty, and suspicious of what people are doing behind my back.\n\nDoctor: When you feel suspicious that people are doing things behind your back, do you think they are actually trying to harm, watch, or interfere with you, or does it feel more like a vague uneasiness without a clear belief?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely a specific belief rather than just a vague uneasiness. I am convinced that dispatch and some of the hospital staff are slipping hidden codes into the radio traffic and chart notes to track my mistakes. I believe they are actively building a case to get me fired.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are deliberately trying to harm, monitor, or sabotage you, and how certain are you that this is really happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am completely certain they are monitoring and trying to sabotage me. For the last couple of months, I've known that dispatch and hospital staff are slipping hidden codes into radio traffic and chart notes to track my mistakes and get me fired. Even though my supervisor swears it's just routine paperwork, I know what they are doing."
